) yield that

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}\left|Dh_{b}\right|^{p}\,dx+\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}|Du|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}+\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}|Du|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}+\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}.

(5.11)

For the second term of (
5.1
), using (
5.7
) and (
5.2
), we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}\left|Dh_{b}-Dw_{b}\right|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}\left|Du\right|^{p}\,dx+\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}\left|Dg\right|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}|Du|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}+\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{2R}}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}.

(5.12)

Finally using (
5.1
) and (
5.9
) together with (
5.10
) and (
5.1
) in (
5.1
), we complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

Let
v_{b}\in W^{1,p}(Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0))
be a weak solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}-\textnormal{div}(\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R}}(x_{1},Dv_{b}))&=0\quad\text{in }Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)\\
v_{b}&=0\quad\text{on }T_{\frac{R}{2}}(0).\end{array}\right.

(5.13)

Then we have the following higher integrability estimate of
Dv_{b}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For any
\tilde{\gamma}_{b}\in(p,\infty)
, there exists a constant
c=c(n,p,\Lambda,\tilde{\gamma}_{b})\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}(x_{0})}}|D\bar{v}|^{\tilde{\gamma}_{b}}\,dx\leq c\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{\frac{R}{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{2}}(x_{0})}}|D\bar{v}|^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\gamma}_{b}}{p}},

(5.14)

where
\bar{v}
is the zero extension of
v_{b}
from
Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)
to
Q_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)
.

Proof.

We abbreviate
\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R}}(x_{1},\xi)=:\bar{A}(x_{1},\xi)
. Define

\displaystyle\hat{v}(x_{1},x^{\prime})=\begin{cases}v(x_{1},x^{\prime})&\text{for }x\in Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0),\\
-v(-x_{1},x^{\prime})&\text{for }x\in Q^{-}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0),\end{cases}

(5.15)

and

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{i}(x_{1},\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\bar{A}_{i}(x_{1},\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})&\text{for}\quad x_{1}\in(0,r)\quad\text{and}\quad 1\leq i\leq n,\\
\bar{A}_{1}(-x_{1},\xi_{1},-\xi^{\prime})&\text{for}\quad x_{1}\in(-r,0)\quad\text{and}\quad i=1,\\
-\bar{A}_{i}(-x_{1},\xi_{1},-\xi^{\prime})&\text{for}\quad x_{1}\in(-r,0)\quad\text{and}\quad i\geq 2.\end{cases}

By the argument of the proofs of
[
46
, Lemma 2.2]
and
[
50
, Theorem 3.4]
, one can see that the vector field
\hat{A}(x_{1},\xi)
satisfies growth condition (
1.9
) and
\hat{v}
is a weak solution of

\displaystyle-\textnormal{div}(\hat{A}(x_{1},D\hat{v}))=0\quad\text{in}\quad Q_{\frac{R}{2}}(0).

Now employing
[
45
, Theorem 2.1]
(or
[
14
, Theorem 3.1]
) and a covering argument, recalling (
5.15
) and then considering that
\bar{v}
is the zero extension of
v_{b}
from
Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)
to
Q_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)
, we obtain (
5.14
).
∎

Now we prove the following comparison estimate.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
and
w_{b}\in W^{1,p}(\Omega_{R})
be the weak solution of (
5.6
) such that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}}}|Dw_{b}|^{p}\,dx\leq c_{1}\lambda^{p}

(5.16)

for some constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(n,p,\Lambda)
.
Then for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists a small number
\delta=\delta(n,p,\Lambda,\varepsilon)
such that if

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)\subset\Omega_{\frac{R}{2}}\subset Q_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)\cap\{x_{1}>-\delta R\}

(5.17)

holds for such
\delta\in(0,1)
, then there exists a weak solution
v_{b}
of
(
5.13
) satisfying

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}|Dv_{b}|^{p}\,dx\leq c_{1}\lambda^{p}

(5.18)

and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}|w_{b}-v_{b}|^{p}\,dx\leq\varepsilon\lambda^{p}R^{p}.

(5.19)

Proof.

Before proving the lemma, we first apply scaling and normalization for problems (
5.6
) and (
5.13
) to use the compactness argument.

Step 1
:
Scaling and normalization
.
For
r>0
, we define the scaled domain as

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}_{r}=\left\{\frac{1}{r}x:x\in\Omega\right\},

a Carathéodory map
\tilde{A}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the normalized function
\tilde{h}_{b}\in W^{1,p}(\Omega_{2R/r})
as

\displaystyle\tilde{A}(x,\xi)=\dfrac{A(rx,\lambda\xi)}{\lambda^{p-1}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tilde{h}_{b}(x)=\frac{h_{b}(rx)}{\lambda\;r}.

Analogous to
\tilde{h}_{b}
, we also define
\tilde{w}_{b}\in\tilde{h}_{b}+W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega_{R/r})
and
\tilde{v}_{b}\in W^{1,p}(Q^{+}_{R/(2r)}(0))
.
Then one can easily obtain the following:

•

\tilde{A}
satisfies the uniform ellipticity and growth conditions as in (
1.9
).

•

\widetilde{w}_{b}
is a solution to the following problem:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}-\textnormal{div}(\tilde{A}_{R/r}(x_{1},D\widetilde{w}_{b}))&=0\quad\text{in}\quad\tilde{\Omega}_{R/r},\\
\ \ \tilde{w}_{b}&=0\quad\text{on}\quad\partial_{w}{\tilde{\Omega}}_{R/r},\end{array}\right.

where

\tilde{A}_{R/r}(x_{1},\xi):=\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptB^{\prime}_{R/r}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{B^{\prime}_{R/r}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{B^{\prime}_{R/r}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{B^{\prime}_{R/r}}}\tilde{A}(x_{1},x^{\prime},z)\,dx^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R}}(y_{1},\xi/\lambda)}{\lambda^{p-1}}.

•

We have

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2r}}(0)\subset\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{R}{2r}}\subset Q_{\frac{R}{2r}}(0)\cap\left\{x_{1}>-\delta\frac{R}{r}\right\}.

•

There holds

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{R/r}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R/r}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R/r}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R/r}}}|D\tilde{w}_{b}|^{p}\,dx\leq c_{1}.

Step 2
: In this step we prove the lemma for
R=1
and
\lambda=1
.
On the contrary suppose that there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and sequences
\{w_{k}\}^{\infty}_{k=1}
and
\{\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}\}^{\infty}_{k=1}
with
\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}:=\tilde{\Omega}_{k}\cap Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)
such that
w_{k}\in W^{1,p}(\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k})
is a weak solution of the following

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}-\textnormal{div}\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{1}}(x_{1},Dw_{k})&=0\quad\text{in }\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k},\\
w_{k}&=0\quad\text{on }\partial_{w}\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}\end{array}\right.

(5.20)

with

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}}}|Dw_{k}|^{p}\,dx\leq c_{1}

(5.21)

and

\displaystyle Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)\subset\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}\subset Q_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)\cap\left\{x_{1}>-1/k\right\},

(5.22)

but

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}|w_{k}-\tilde{v}|^{p}\,dx>\varepsilon_{0}

(5.23)

holds for any weak solution
\tilde{v}\in W^{1,p}(Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}-\textnormal{div}\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{1}}(x_{1},D\tilde{v})&=0\quad\text{in }Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0),\\
\tilde{v}&=0\quad\text{on }T_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)\end{array}\right.

with

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}|D\tilde{v}|^{p}\,dx\leq c_{1}.

(5.24)

With the aid of (
5.21
) and (
5.22
), the sequence
\{w_{k}\}^{\infty}_{k=1}
is uniformly bounded in
W^{1,p}(Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
. Hence, up to a subsequence, there is
w_{0}\in W^{1,p}(Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0))
such that

\displaystyle\begin{cases}w_{k}\rightharpoonup w_{0}\quad\text{weakly in }W^{1,p}(Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)),\\
w_{k}\rightarrow w_{0}\quad\text{in }L^{p}(Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)).\end{cases}

(5.25)

Then together with (
5.20
) and (
5.22
), we notice that
w_{0}=0
on
T_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)
in the trace sense. Moreover, using the method of
Browder and Minty
as in
[
18
, Lemma 4.5]
, we see that
w_{0}
is a weak solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}-\textnormal{div}\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{1}}(x_{1},Dw_{0})&=0\quad\text{in }Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0),\\
w_{0}&=0\quad\text{on }T_{\frac{1}{2}}(0).\end{array}\right.

On the other hand, from (
5.21
) and (
5.25
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)}}|Dw_{0}|^{p}\,dx\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\tilde{\Omega}_{\frac{1}{2},k}}}|Dw_{k}|^{p}\,dx\leq c_{1}.

(5.26)

Then one can get a contradiction by taking
k
sufficiently large and comparing (
5.23
)–(
5.24
) to (
5.25
)–(
5.26
). Thus we have shown Lemma
5.4
for
R=1
and
\lambda=1
.

Step 3
. We scale back the result of Step 2 to obtain the required result of the lemma for general
R>0
and
\lambda\geq 1
. Indeed, setting
w_{b}(x)=\lambda R\tilde{w}(x/R)
,
v_{b}(x)=\lambda R\tilde{v}(x/R)
and
\Omega_{R}=R\tilde{\Omega}_{R}
, we get (
5.18
) and (
5.19
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Finally, we show third comparison estimate as follows.

Lemma 5.5
.

Under the same assumptions of Lemma
5.4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}}|Dw_{b}-D\bar{v}|^{p}\,dx\leq\varepsilon\lambda^{p},

where
\bar{v}
is the zero extension of
v_{b}
from
Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)
to
Q_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.4
, we see that for any
\varepsilon_{1}>0
, there exist a small
\delta_{1}=\delta_{1}(n,\Lambda,\varepsilon_{1})
and a weak solution
v_{b}\in W^{1,p}(Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0))
of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}-\textnormal{div}\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{\frac{R}{2}}}(x_{1},Dv_{b})&=0\quad\text{in }Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0),\\
v_{b}&=0\quad\text{on }T_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)\end{array}\right.

with

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}|Dv_{b}|^{p}\,dx\leq c\lambda^{p}

such that if (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) are true for
\delta_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{+}_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)}}|w_{b}-v_{b}|^{p}\,dx\leq\varepsilon_{1}\lambda^{p}R^{p}.

(5.27)

One can see that
\bar{v}
is a weak solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rlll}-\textnormal{div}\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R}}(x_{1},D\bar{v})&=-D_{1}(\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R},n}(0,Dv(0,x^{\prime}))\chi_{\{x_{1}<0\}})\quad\text{in }\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}},\\
\bar{v}&=0\quad\text{on }\partial\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}.\end{array}\right.

(5.28)

Choose a cut-off function
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(Q_{\frac{3R}{8}})
such that

\displaystyle\varphi=1\,\,\text{on}\,\,Q_{\frac{R}{4}},\,\,0\leq\varphi\leq 1\,\,\text{and}\,\,|D\varphi|\leq\frac{c}{R}\,\,\text{in}\,\,Q_{\frac{3R}{8}}.

(5.29)

Testing
(\tilde{w}-\bar{v})\varphi^{p}\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}})
for (
5.6
) and (
5.28
) implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{1}&:=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}\left(\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R}}(x_{1},Dw_{b})-\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R}}(x_{1},D\bar{v})\right)\cdot(D[(w_{b}-\bar{v})\varphi^{p}])\,dx\\
&=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)}\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R},n}(0,D\bar{v}(0,x^{\prime}))D_{1}[(w_{b}-\bar{v})\varphi^{p}]\,dx=:I_{2}.\end{split}

(5.30)

By Young’s inequality and (
1.9
), we get

\displaystyle I_{1}\geq c^{*}\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}|Dw_{b}-D\bar{v}|^{p}\varphi^{p}\,dx-c\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}|w_{b}-\bar{v}|^{p}|D\varphi|^{p}\,dx

(5.31)

with
c^{*}=c^{*}(n,p,\Lambda)\in(0,1)
and
c=c(n,p,\Lambda)\geq 1
. Furthermore, using Hölder’s inequality and Sobolev embedding, we discover that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)}|w_{b}-\bar{v}|^{p}|D\varphi|^{p}\,dx&=\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)}|w_{b}|^{p}|D\varphi|^{p}\,dx\\
&\leq\frac{c}{R^{p}}\left(\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)}|w_{b}|^{p^{*}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{p}{p^{*}}}|\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)|^{\frac{p}{n}}\\
&\leq\left(\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}|Dw_{b}|^{p}\,dx\right)|\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)|^{\frac{p}{n}}.\end{split}

(5.32)

Merging (
5.27
), (
5.31
) and (
5.32
), it follows that

\displaystyle I_{1}\geq c^{*}\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}|Dw_{b}-D\bar{v}|^{p}\varphi^{p}\,dx-\big(c\varepsilon_{1}\lambda^{p}+c\delta^{\frac{p}{n}}\lambda^{p}\big)|\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}|,

(5.33)

where we have also used (
5.16
), (
5.17
) and (
5.29
).
For
I_{2}
, we use (
1.9
), (
5.7
) and (
5.29
) together with Hölder’s inequality and Poincaré’s inequality to find that

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)}|\bar{A}_{B^{\prime}_{R}}(0,Dv_{b}(0,x^{\prime}))|\;|D_{1}[\varphi(w_{b}-\bar{v})]|\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)}|Dv_{b}(0,x^{\prime})|^{p-1}(R^{-1}|w_{b}-\tilde{v}|+|D(w_{b}-\tilde{v})|)\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)}|Dv_{b}(0,x^{\prime})|^{2p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\left(\int_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)}(R^{-1}|w_{b}|+|Dw_{b}|)^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}|\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)|^{\frac{1}{2p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}}|Dw_{b}|^{p}\,dx\right)|\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}\setminus Q^{+}_{\frac{3R}{8}}(0)|\leq c\delta\lambda^{p}|\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}|,

(5.34)

where on the second last inequality we have also used Lemma
5.3
.
Combining (
5.17
), (
5.29
), (
5.30
), (
5.33
) and (
5.1
), we discover

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{R}{4}}}}|Dw_{b}-D\bar{v}|^{p}\,dx\leq\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{3R}{8}}}}|Dw_{b}-D\bar{v}|^{p}\varphi^{p}\,dx\leq c\left(\varepsilon_{1}+\delta^{\frac{p}{n}}+\delta\right)\lambda^{p}\leq\varepsilon\lambda^{p}

by choosing
\varepsilon_{1}
and then
\delta\in(0,\delta_{1}]
small enough. This completes the proof.
∎

5.2
.
Proof of Theorem
1.6

Fix
x_{0}\in\Omega
and
\rho_{0}\in(0,1)
such that
Q_{2\rho_{0}}(x_{0})\subset{\Omega^{\prime}}
. With
\gamma>p
and
Q_{R}(x)\subset Q_{2\rho_{0}}(x_{0})
, we define the following functional:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\delta}(x,R)

\displaystyle:=\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}(x)}}|Du|^{p}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{R}(x)}}(|F|^{p}+1)^{1+\sigma}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p(1+\sigma)}}+\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{R}(x)}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p(1+\sigma)}}

for
0<\sigma<\min\{1,\frac{\gamma-p}{p}\}
small enough given by Lemma
5.2
, and for some
\delta\in(0,1)
which will be determined later. We also define

\displaystyle{\rm Tail}(x,R):=

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{k=1}^{l}\beta_{k}\Bigg(\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{3^{k}R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{3^{k}R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{3^{k}R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{3^{k}R}(x)}}|Du|^{p}\,dy\right)^{\frac{\theta}{p}}+\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ_{3^{k}R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{3^{k}R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{3^{k}R}(x)}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{3^{k}R}(x)}}|Dg|^{p}\,dy\right)^{\frac{\theta}{p}}\Bigg)\right]^{\frac{1}{\theta}}

\displaystyle+\left(R^{1-\varrho}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus Q_{3^{l}R}(x)}\dfrac{|u(y)-(u)_{3^{l}R}|^{q-1}}{|y-x|^{n+sq}}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{m-1}},

where
l=l(R)\in\mathbb{N}
is such that
\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}\leq 3^{l}R<\rho_{0}
, and
\varrho<m-qs
is any number with
m:=\min\{p,q\}
. Moreover, we set

\displaystyle\Xi(x,R):=\Psi_{\delta}(x,R)+{\rm Tail}(x,R)\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\Xi_{0}(x,R):=\Psi_{1}(x,R)+\left(R^{1-\varrho}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus Q_{R}(x)}\dfrac{|u(y)-(u)_{R}|^{q-1}}{|y-x|^{n+sq}}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{m-1}}.

Fix
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
such that
\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq\rho_{0}
. Then, we define

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}:=\sup\bigg\{\Xi(x,R)\ :\ x\in\Omega_{r_{1}}(x_{0}),\ \frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{729}<R\leq\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}\bigg\}.

(5.35)

Now the proof is divided into five steps as below.

Step 1
:
Upper bound on
\lambda_{1}
.
Similar to Step 1 of Proposition
3.4
, it is easy to deduce that

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\leq\frac{c}{\delta}\left(\frac{2\rho_{0}}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{2n+sq}{m-1}+\frac{n+p}{p}}\,\Xi_{0}(x_{0},2\rho_{0}),

(5.36)

where
c=c(\texttt{data}_{2})
is a constant.

Step 2
:
Vitali covering
. For
\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}
and
T>0
, we set

\displaystyle E_{\lambda}^{T}:=\Big\{x\in\Omega_{T}(x_{0})\,:\,\sup_{0\leq R\leq\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{729}}\Psi_{\delta}(x,R)>\lambda\Big\}.

From (
5.35
), for each
x\in\Omega_{r_{1}}(x_{0})
and
R\in\big[\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{729},\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}\big]
, there holds

\displaystyle\Psi_{\delta}(x,R)\leq\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda.

Moreover, for each
x\in E_{\lambda}^{r_{1}}
with
\lambda\geq\lambda_{1}
, there exists a radius
R_{x}\in(0,\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{729}\big]
such that

\displaystyle\Psi_{\delta}(x,R_{x})\geq\lambda\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Psi_{\delta}(x,R)\leq\lambda\quad\mbox{for all }R\in(R_{x},\tfrac{\rho_{0}}{3}\big].

(5.37)

By Vitali’s covering lemma, there exists a sequence of disjoint cylinders
\{Q_{R_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\}
such that

\displaystyle E_{\lambda}^{r_{1}}\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{5R_{x_{j}}}(x_{j}).

(5.38)

Setting
R_{j}:=R_{x_{j}}
and
Q_{j}:=Q_{R_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})
, for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
, we choose
l_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}\leq 3^{l_{j}}R_{j}<\rho_{0}.

(5.39)

Since
R_{j}\leq\frac{2}{729}\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}
, it is clear that
l_{j}\geq 5
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
.

Step 3
:
The comparison estimates
. We set
\varsigma\Omega_{j}:=\Omega_{\varsigma R_{j}}
and
\varsigma Q_{j}:=Q_{\varsigma R_{j}}
, for
\varsigma>0
.
Let
h_{j}
be the weak solution to problem (
5.1
) with
R=27R_{j}
and
x_{0}=x_{j}
, there. Then, from (
5.1
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}|V(Du)-V(Dh_{j})|^{2}\,dx\\
&\leq(\varepsilon+c(\nu)R_{j}^{\Bbbk})\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt81\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81\Omega_{j}}}|Du|^{p}\,dx+c(\varepsilon,\nu)\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt81\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81\Omega_{j}}}(|F|^{p}+1)\,dx\\
&\quad+c(\varepsilon,\nu)\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt81Q_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81Q_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81Q_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{81Q_{j}}}|Dg|^{p}\,dx+\nu\left(R_{j}^{1-\varrho}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus 3^{l_{j}+3}Q_{j}}\dfrac{|u(y)-(u)_{3^{l_{j}+3}R_{j}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}\,dy\right)^{\frac{p}{m-1}}\\
&\quad+\nu\left[\sum^{l_{j}}_{k=2}\beta_{k}\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt3^{k+3}Q_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{3^{k+3}Q_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{3^{k+3}Q_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{3^{k+3}Q_{j}}}|Dg|^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{\theta}{p}}+\beta_{k}\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt3^{k+3}\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{3^{k+3}\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{3^{k+3}\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{3^{k+3}\Omega_{j}}}|Du|^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{\theta}{p}}\right]^{\frac{p}{\theta}},\end{split}

where the constants
\varrho,m,\theta,\tau,\Bbbk
and
\beta_{k}
are as in Lemma
4.2
and
l_{j}\geq 5
is given by (
5.39
). Proceeding as in Step 3 of the proof of Proposition
3.4
(thanks to (
5.37
)), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}|V(Du)-V(Dh_{j})|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq(\varepsilon+c(\nu)R_{j}^{\Bbbk})\lambda^{p}+c(\varepsilon,\nu)\lambda^{p}\delta^{p}+\nu c_{\beta}\lambda^{p}+\nu\lambda^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(\varepsilon+c(\nu)R_{j}^{\Bbbk})\lambda^{p}+c(\varepsilon,\nu)\lambda^{p}\delta^{p}+\nu c\lambda^{p}

\displaystyle=:S_{1}(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,R_{j})\lambda^{p},

(5.40)

where
c=c(\texttt{data}_{2},\gamma)
and
c_{\beta}\simeq(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\beta_{k})^{\frac{p}{\theta}}\leq c<\infty
.
Next, for the above
h_{j}
, let
w_{j}
be the weak solution to problem (
5.6
) with
R=27R_{j}
and
x_{0}=x_{j}
. Then, by the energy estimates (
5.2
) and (
5.7
), we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt27\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{27\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{27\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{27\Omega_{j}}}|Dw_{j}|^{p}\,dx\leq c_{1}\lambda^{p}.

(5.41)

Moreover, from Lemma
5.2
and Proposition
3.4
(for
\rho_{0}=54R_{j}
and
x_{0}=x_{j}
, there), we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt27\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{27\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{27\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{27\Omega_{j}}}|V(Dw_{j})-V(Dh_{j})|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\;\delta^{\frac{\sigma}{p^{\prime}(1+\sigma)}}\left[\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}|Du|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}+c\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{54\Omega_{j}}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq c\;\delta^{\frac{\sigma}{p^{\prime}(1+\sigma)}}\left[\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}|Du|^{p}\,dx+\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}(|F|^{p}+1)^{1+\sigma}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{(1+\sigma)}}+\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{162\Omega_{j}}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\sigma}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+R_{j}^{(1-\varrho)p^{\prime}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus 162Q_{j}}\dfrac{|u(y)-(u)_{162R_{j}}|^{q-1}}{|y-x_{0}|^{n+sq}}\,dy\right)^{\frac{p}{m-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq c\;\delta^{\frac{\sigma}{p^{\prime}(1+\sigma)}}\lambda^{p},

(5.42)

where on the last line we used the exit-time condition (
5.37
).
Now we consider two cases:

\displaystyle 27Q_{R_{j}}\not\subset\Omega\quad\text{or}\quad 27Q_{R_{j}}\subset\Omega.

If
27Q_{R_{j}}\not\subset\Omega
holds, then there exists a point
y_{0}\in\partial\Omega\cap 27Q_{R_{j}}
. By the assumption on
\Omega
as in Definition
1.5
, there exists a new coordinate system
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}
such that in this coordinate system
y_{0}
is the origin and there holds

\displaystyle Q_{\frac{27}{4}R_{j}}(0)\cap\{x_{1}>\tfrac{27}{4}\delta R_{j}\}\subset\Omega\cap Q_{\frac{27}{4}R_{j}}(0)\subset Q_{\frac{27}{4}R_{j}}(0)\cap\{x_{1}>-\tfrac{27}{4}\delta R_{j}\}.

Let the corresponding
v_{j}
be as in Lemma
5.4
; i.e., solution to problem (
5.13
) defined in
Q_{\frac{27}{4}R_{j}}(0)\cap\{x_{1}>\tfrac{27}{4}\delta R_{j}\}
and extended by
0
in
Q_{\frac{27}{4}R_{j}}(0)\cap\{x_{1}\leq\tfrac{27}{4}\delta R_{j}\}
.
Eventually, by (
5.2
), (
5.41
), (
5.2
) and Lemma
5.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}|V(Du)-V(Dv_{j})|^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq S(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,R_{j})\lambda^{p},

(5.43)

where

\displaystyle S(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,R_{j}):=\varepsilon+c(\nu)R_{j}^{\Bbbk}+c(\varepsilon,\nu)\delta^{p}+c_{*}\;\delta^{\frac{\sigma}{p^{\prime}(1+\sigma)}}+c_{*}\nu

with
c(\nu)=c(\nu,\texttt{data}_{2},\gamma)
,
c(\varepsilon,\nu)=c(\varepsilon,\nu,\texttt{data}_{2},\gamma)
, and
c_{*}=c_{*}(\texttt{data}_{2},\gamma)
being universal constants. If
27Q_{R_{j}}\subset\Omega
holds, then we proceed with
w_{j}
instead of
v_{j}
and the estimate (
5.43
) holds with
w_{j}
replaced by
v_{j}
from the results of
[
45
,
14
]
.

Step 4
:
Estimate involving level sets
. We first obtain some uniform estimates for
|\Omega_{j}|
. On account of (
5.37
), similar to Section 3, we have the occurrence of at least one of (
3.30
) and (
3.33
). Therefore, after some elementary simplifications, we get

\displaystyle|\Omega_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c(p)}{\lambda^{p}}\int_{\Omega_{j}\cap\{|Du|\geq\lambda/4\}}|Du|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{c(p)}{(\delta\lambda)^{p(1+\sigma)}}\left[\int_{\Omega_{j}\cap\{(|F|^{p}+1)^{1/p}\geq\lambda/8\}}\big(|F|^{p}+1\big)^{1+\sigma}\,dx+\int_{Q_{j}\cap\{|Dg|\geq\lambda/8\}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right].

(5.44)

Next, we recall the following inequality (see
[
45
, Lemma 6.1]
):

\displaystyle\text{Let }\xi,\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\text{ be such that }|\xi|\geq\kappa\text{ for some }\kappa>0.\text{ Then for all }\alpha\geq p,

\displaystyle\qquad|\xi|^{p}\leq c|\xi-\zeta|^{p}+c(\alpha)\kappa^{p-\alpha}|\zeta|^{\alpha}.

Consequently, if
27Q_{R_{j}}\not\subset\Omega
holds, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{5\Omega_{j}\cap\{|Du|\geq\kappa\lambda\}}|Du|^{p}\,dx\leq c\int_{5\Omega_{j}\cap\{|Du|\geq\kappa\lambda\}}\big(|Du-Dv_{j}|^{p}+(\kappa\lambda)^{-\gamma}|Dv_{j}|^{p+\gamma}\big)\,dx.

(5.45)

Moreover, from the
L^{\gamma}
estimate of Lemma
5.3
and the exit-time condition (
5.37
), we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt5\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{5\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{5\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{5\Omega_{j}}}|Dv_{j}|^{p+\gamma}\right)^{\frac{1}{p+\gamma}}\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}|Dv_{j}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}|Du|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\left(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{6\Omega_{j}}}|Du-Dv_{j}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\lambda,

(5.46)

where we have also used the estimate of (
5.43
) and (
2.8
).
Therefore, (
5.2
) and (
5.45
) imply that

\displaystyle\int_{5\Omega_{j}\cap\{|Du|\geq\kappa\lambda\}}|Du|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\big(S(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,R_{j})+\kappa^{-\gamma}\big)\lambda^{p}|5\Omega_{j}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(S(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,R_{j})+\kappa^{-\gamma}\big)\left[\int_{\Omega_{j}\cap\{|Du|\geq\lambda/4\}}|Du|^{p}\,dx+\frac{|\Omega_{j}|}{|Q_{j}|}\frac{\lambda^{-p\sigma}}{\delta^{p(1+\sigma)}}\int_{Q_{j}\cap\{|Dg|\geq\lambda/8\}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\frac{\lambda^{-p\sigma}}{\delta^{p(1+\sigma)}}\int_{\Omega_{j}\cap\{(|F|^{p}+1)^{1/p}\geq\lambda/8\}}\big(|F|^{p}+1\big)^{1+\sigma}\,dx\right].

On the other hand, if
27Q_{R_{j}}\subset\Omega
holds, (
5.45
) and (
5.46
) are obtained with
Dw_{j}
instead of
Dv_{j}
and so we have the above estimate again. Since
\{Q_{j}\}
is a disjoint collection such that each
Q_{j}
is contained in
Q_{r_{2}}(x_{0})
, the above expression and (
5.38
) yield

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{r_{1}}(x_{0})\cap\{|Du|\geq\kappa\lambda\}}|Du|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\int_{5\Omega_{j}\cap\{|Du|\geq\kappa\lambda\}}|Du|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(S(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,\rho_{0})+\kappa^{-\gamma}\big)\left[\int_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})\cap\{|Du|>\kappa\lambda\}}|Du|^{p}\,dx+\frac{\lambda^{-p\sigma}}{\delta^{p(1+\sigma)}}\int_{Q_{r_{2}}(x_{0})\cap\{|Dg|>\hat{\kappa}\lambda\}}|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}\,dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\frac{\lambda^{-p\sigma}}{\delta^{p(1+\sigma)}}\int_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})\cap\{(|F|^{p}+1)^{1/p}>\tilde{\kappa}\lambda\}}\big(|F|^{p}+1\big)^{1+\sigma}\,dx\right].

(5.47)

Step 5
:
Conclusion
. For
t\geq 0
, we define the truncated function

\displaystyle[|Du|]_{t}:=\min\{|Du|,t\}.

Then, for
t\geq\lambda_{1}
, from (
5.2
), we easily obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}\lambda^{\gamma-1-p}

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{r_{1}}(x_{0})\cap\{|Du|\geq\kappa\lambda\}}|Du|^{p}\,dxd\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(S(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,\rho_{0})+\kappa^{-\gamma}\big)\left[\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}\lambda^{\gamma-1-p}\int_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})\cap\{|Du|>\kappa\lambda\}}|Du|^{p}\,dxd\lambda\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}\lambda^{\gamma-1-p(1+\sigma)}\int_{Q_{r_{2}}(x_{0})\cap\{|Dg|>\hat{\kappa}\lambda\}}\frac{|Dg|^{p(1+\sigma)}}{\delta^{p(1+\sigma)}}\,dxd\lambda\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\int_{\lambda_{1}}^{t}\lambda^{\gamma-1-p(1+\sigma)}\int_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})\cap\{(|F|^{p}+1)^{1/p}>\tilde{\kappa}\lambda\}}\frac{(|F|^{p}+1)^{1+\sigma}}{\delta^{p(1+\sigma)}}\,dxd\lambda\right].

Now, by Fubini’s theorem and a simple change of variable (see, e.g.,
[
24
]
), we can deduce that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{r_{1}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{1}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{1}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{1}}(x_{0})}}

\displaystyle[|Du|]_{t}^{\gamma-p}|Du|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\big(S(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,\rho_{0})+\kappa^{-\gamma}\big)\left[\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}[|Du|]_{t}^{\gamma-p}|Du|^{p}\,dx+\ \mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}\frac{|Dg|^{\gamma}}{\delta^{\gamma}}\,dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{r_{2}}(x_{0})}}\frac{(|F|^{p}+1)^{\gamma/p}}{\delta^{\gamma}}\,dx\right]+c\kappa^{\gamma}\lambda_{1}^{\gamma},

(5.48)

where
c
depends only on
\texttt{data}_{2}
and
\gamma
.
We choose
\kappa=\kappa(\texttt{data}_{2},\gamma)>1
large enough,
\varepsilon,\nu\in(0,1)
,
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
small enough depending only on
\texttt{data}_{2}
, and then
\gamma
, and
r_{*}\in(0,1)
small enough depending on
\texttt{data}_{2},\gamma
and
\nu
(in the same order) such that

\displaystyle c\kappa^{-\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{16},\;\;c\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{16},\;\;c\nu\leq\frac{1}{16},\;\;c_{*}c(\varepsilon,\nu)\delta^{\frac{\sigma}{p^{\prime}(1+\sigma)}}\leq\frac{1}{16}\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;c_{*}c(\nu)r_{*}^{\Bbbk}\leq\frac{1}{16}.

Consequently, for all
\rho_{0}\leq r_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle S(\varepsilon,\nu,\delta,\rho_{0})+\kappa^{-\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{2}

and noting (
5.36
), by a standard iteration argument, we deduce from (
5.2
) that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\frac{\rho_{0}}{3}}(x_{0})}}

\displaystyle[|Du|]_{t}^{\gamma-p}|Du|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq c\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ_{\rho_{0}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{\rho_{0}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{\rho_{0}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q_{\rho_{0}}(x_{0})}}|Dg|^{\gamma}\,dx+\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773pt\Omega_{\rho_{0}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\rho_{0}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\rho_{0}}(x_{0})}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{\Omega_{\rho_{0}}(x_{0})}}(|F|^{p}+1)^{\gamma/p}\,dx+c\,\Xi_{0}(x_{0},2\rho_{0})^{\gamma}.

Taking the limit as
t\to\infty
in the above expression, by a standard covering argument as in the proof of Proposition
3.4
, we obtain the required result of Theorem
1.6
(where we also use
p(1+\sigma)<\gamma
and Hölder’s inequality in
\Xi_{0}(x_{0},2\rho_{0})
). ∎
